  1. Root cause

  That exceeds the approved pre-Phase-40 metadata boundary.                     
                                                                                
  2. Required sealing architecture                                              
                                                                                
  Do not determine whether an artifact is sealed by inspecting:                 
                                                                                
  family                                                                        
  condition                                                                     
  answer_type                                                                   
  question                                                                      
  status                                                                        
  filename keywords                                                             
  record contents                                                               
                                                                                
  The preferred architecture is:                                                
                                                                                
  trusted sealed-artifact manifest / registry                                   
          ↓                                                                     
  canonical artifact identity + integrity hash                                  
          ↓                                                                     
  authorization/classification BEFORE parsing records                           
          ↓                                                                     
  dev-facing APIs either allow or refuse access                                 
                                                                                
  The sealing decision must attach to the artifact, not to what the artifact    
  says about itself.                                                            
                                                                                
  Phase 8 should implement this mechanism using synthetic fixtures only.        
                                                                                
  Do not create or inspect any real test/Q42 artifact.                          
                                                                                
  3. Classification must happen before record parsing                           
                                                                                
  load_dev_results() must decide whether access is permitted before             
  opening/parsing evaluation records.                                           
                                                                                
  The forbidden pattern is:                                                     
                                                                                
  open result records                                                           
  → inspect family/content                                                      
  → decide whether they were sealed                                             
                                                                                
  The required pattern is:                                                      
                                                                                
  identify artifact                                                             
  → establish trusted classification                                            
  → if sealed: refuse                                                           
  → only if authorized dev artifact: parse records                              
                                                                                
  This is a hard requirement.                                                   
                                                                                
  4. Manifest/provenance-based classification

  1. Blocking defect reproduced by independent review                           
                                                                                
  The new registry-based design correctly removed:                              
                                                                                
  family-based sealing                                                          
  unknown-family → dev                                                          
  sealed-record parsing for metadata                                            
                                                                                
  However, the new authority is still effectively caller-controlled.            
                                                                                
  An ordinary caller can currently do the equivalent of:                        
                                                                                
  registry = ArtifactRegistry()                                                 
                                                                                
  registry.register(                                                            
      known_sealed_path,                                                        
      artifact_kind="dev",                                                      
      ...                                                                       
  )                                                                             
                                                                                
  load_dev_results(..., registry=registry)                                      
                                                                                
  and successfully load the known sealed bytes.                                 
                                                                                
  Therefore the security boundary has moved from:                               
                                                                                
  caller-controlled family                                                      
                                                                                
  to:                                                                           
                                                                                
  caller-controlled registry declaration                                        
                                                                                
  That is still insufficient.                                                   
                                                                                
  The registry is described as:                                                 
                                                                                
  trusted                                                                       
                                                                                
  but the implementation does not yet establish or enforce where that trust     
  comes from.

  The final Phase 8 boundary must satisfy:                                      
                                                                                
  trusted/frozen artifact authority                                             
          ↓                                                                     
  artifact identity + artifact kind                                             
          ↓                                                                     
  ordinary dev/report code may QUERY classification                             
          ↓                                                                     
  ordinary dev/report code may NOT redefine classification                      
                                                                                
  Specifically:                                                                 
                                                                                
  known sealed artifact                                                         
                                                                                
  must remain sealed even if ordinary caller code attempts to:                  
                                                                                
  create another registry                                                       
  register the same path as dev                                                 
  register the same SHA as dev

  "if path contains sealed..."

  check and still measures nothing."
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ref: README Ph22
  wording: "Defining constructs (GROUP BY, LIMIT, argmax/top-k) appear 0 times
  across all relevant training gold — checked in D's SQL text and in C's
  metadata."
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ref: README Ph23
  wording: "Training must already contain the required join arity and constructs

  through other combinations." / "Superset rule. For each held-out set H and
  every training set T: assert not H ⊆ T."
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ref: README Ph27
  wording: "Few-shot examples are train-side only · no held-out entity, value,
  operation or combination · no logical fingerprint shared with any probe
  item."
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  ref: CLAUDE §16
  wording: minimum support · per-value collateral-loss limits · cumulative
  attribute-level coverage floors · per-split support reporting ·
  operation-heldout construct exclusion · compositional superset exclusion ·
  cross-probe consistency

  ---

  1. Support-unit decision

  The protocol uses both units deliberately, in different columns of the same 
  CSV. README:700 defines supporting_companies as "how many companies carry it";
  README:704 defines remaining_attribute_coverage as "rows still carrying that
  attribute in training". They are not required to share a unit.

  Empirical resolution — README's frozen table reproduces ROW occurrences 
  exactly:

  ┌────────────┬────────┬──────┬─────┬─────┬─────┬─────┬─────┬─────┬─────────┐
  │            │        │ valu │     │     │     │ 6–1 │ 16– │     │  band   │
  │   field    │  unit  │  es  │ s1  │ s2  │ 3–5 │  5  │ 40  │ >40 │ candida │
  │            │        │      │     │     │     │     │     │     │   tes   │
  ├────────────┼────────┼──────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────────┤
  │ processes  │ rows   │   49 │   9 │   3 │  11 │  15 │   9 │   2 │    26 ✓ │
  ├────────────┼────────┼──────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────────┤
  │ processes  │ compan │   49 │  11 │   3 │   9 │  15 │   9 │   2 │    24 ✗ │
  │            │ ies    │      │     │     │     │     │     │     │         │
  ├────────────┼────────┼──────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────────┤
  │ services   │ rows   │   22 │   5 │   2 │   7 │   6 │   1 │   1 │    13 ✓ │
  ├────────────┼────────┼──────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────────┤
  │ services   │ compan │   22 │   5 │   2 │   7 │   6 │   1 │   1 │    13 ✓ │
  │            │ ies    │      │     │     │     │     │     │     │         │
  ├────────────┼────────┼──────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────────┤
  │ certificat │ rows   │   59 │  36 │   7 │   3 │   5 │   4 │   4 │     8 ✓ │
  │ ions       │        │      │     │     │     │     │     │     │         │
  ├────────────┼────────┼──────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────┼─────────┤
  │ certificat │ compan │   59 │  36 │   8 │   2 │   5 │   4 │   4 │     7 ✗ │
  │ ions       │ ies    │      │     │     │     │     │     │     │         │
  └────────────┴────────┴──────┴─────┴─────┴─────┴─────┴─────┴─────┴─────────┘

  README Phase 21's frozen table is 49/9/3/11/15/9/2 · 22/5/2/7/6/1/1 · 
  59/36/7/3/5/4/4 — the row rows match on every cell; the company rows do not.

  Only three values differ in eligibility:

  ┌────────────────┬─────────────────────────┬──────┬───────────┬──────────┐
  │     field      │          value          │ rows │ companies │   note   │
  ├────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼──────┼───────────┼──────────┤
  │ processes      │ Converter Manufacturing │    3 │         1 │ row-only │
  ├────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼──────┼───────────┼──────────┤
  │ processes      │ Inverter Manufacturing  │    3 │         1 │ row-only │
  ├────────────────┼─────────────────────────┼──────┼───────────┼──────────┤
  │ certifications │ ASI Chain of Custody    │    3 │         2 │ row-only │
  └────────────────┴─────────────────────────┴──────┴───────────┴──────────┘

  Interpretation analysis

  - A — eligibility = rows, collateral reporting = companies. Reproduces
    README's frozen table and both CSV column definitions verbatim. Internally
    consistent. Risk: a support-3 value living in one company is nominally
    eligible.
  - B — both = exact companies. Contradicts README's frozen numeric table
    (24/13/7 ≠ 26/13/8), which would mean the protocol's own published counts
    are wrong. Scientifically cleaner for "support", but requires treating a
    frozen table as erroneous.
  - C — rows for eligibility, plus an explicit company floor. Same as A with an
    added knob.

  Recommendation: interpretation A, because it is the only one that reproduces
  the frozen table and both column definitions without declaring part of the
  protocol mistaken. C's extra knob is unnecessary: Phase 3 guarantees every row
  of a company sits on one split side, so a single-company value has all its
  support on one side and is automatically removed by any rule requiring train ≥
  1 AND test ≥ 1. Verified — Converter Manufacturing and Inverter Manufacturing
  both have train=0, and are excluded.

  Leakage/generalization consequence: none differs between A and B for the
  selection actually proposed below — the sensitivity run shows identical
  selections under both units.

  ---

  2. Complete candidate support / cost summary

  141 · certifications 123 / 116.

  Under the omit-the-item policy a held-out value removes the entire attribute
  from every train row carrying it, so train rows lost is the collateral unit.

  ---

  3. Coverage-floor analysis

  Feasible k-combinations (union-based, so a row carrying two held-out values is
  lost once) under split rule train≥1, test≥1:

  ┌────────────────┬─────┬──────┬──────┬──────┬──────┬──────┬──────────┐
  │   attribute    │  k  │ @70% │ @75% │ @80% │ @85% │ @90% │ best cov │
  ├────────────────┼─────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────────┤
  │ processes      │   2 │  210 │  210 │  210 │  205 │  159 │    98.6% │
  ├────────────────┼─────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────────┤
  │ processes      │   4 │ 5985 │ 5955 │ 5569 │ 4192 │ 1368 │    96.6% │
  ├────────────────┼─────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────────┤
  │ services       │   3 │  165 │  165 │  165 │  160 │   99 │    98.6% │
  ├────────────────┼─────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────────┤
  │ services       │   4 │  330 │  330 │  330 │  277 │  118 │    97.3% │
  ├────────────────┼─────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────────┤
  │ certifications │   2 │    6 │    6 │    6 │    6 │    4 │    95.1% │
  ├────────────────┼─────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────────┤
  │ certifications │   3 │    4 │    4 │    4 │    4 │    1 │    90.2% │
  ├────────────────┼─────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────┼──────────┤
  │ certifications │   4 │    1 │    1 │    1 │    0 │    0 │    84.6% │
  └────────────────┴─────┴──────┴──────┴──────┴──────┴──────┴──────────┘

  The coverage floor is not the binding constraint — processes and services stay
  above 91% even at k=4, because each row carries several values and only rows
  containing a held-out value lose the attribute. The binding constraint is
  certification pool size.

  Recommendation: 85%. It is meaningful (it forbids certifications at k=4, cov
  84.6%), it is comfortably satisfied by every sensible processes/services
  choice, and the sensitivity run shows the selection is identical at 80% and
  85% — so it is not a knife-edge. 90% would begin dictating the selection
  rather than bounding it, which inverts the intended role of a floor.

  ---

  4. remaining_attribute_coverage threshold

  README:706 (per-value eligibility) and README:712-713 (cumulative attribute
  floor) are two scopes of the same quantity — "rows still carrying that
  attribute in training" — not two different concepts. Nothing in the protocol
  requires different values.

  Recommendation: one number, 85%, applied at both scopes. Per-value it is never
  binding (the worst single value, ISO/IEC 17025, leaves 93.5%); cumulatively
  it is the operative constraint. A second independent knob would be a redundant
  degree of freedom with no protocol basis.

  ---

  5. Value-holdout count analysis

  Pool sizes differ by 5× (26 / 13 / 8 before split filtering; 20 / 11 / 4 after
  train≥2, test≥1), so equal counts are inappropriate.

  ┌────────────────┬───────┬───────────┬───────────┬───────────┬────────────┐
  │   attribute    │ pool  │    k=1    │    k=2    │    k=3    │    k=4     │
  │                │ (2/1) │           │           │           │            │
  ├────────────────┼───────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────┼────────────┤
  │ processes      │    20 │ trivially │ feasible  │ feasible  │ feasible,  │
  │                │       │  feasible │           │           │ cov 88.5%  │
  ├────────────────┼───────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────┼────────────┤
  │                │       │           │           │ feasible, │ feasible,  │
  │ services       │    11 │ feasible  │ feasible  │  cov      │ cov 97.3%  │
  │                │       │           │           │ 92.6%     │            │
  ├────────────────┼───────┼───────────┼───────────┼───────────┼────────────┤
  │                │       │           │ feasible, │ feasible, │ infeasible │
  │ certifications │     4 │ feasible  │  cov      │  cov      │  @85%      │
  │                │       │           │ 95.1%     │ 90.2%     │            │
  └────────────────┴───────┴───────────┴───────────┴───────────┴────────────┘

  Recommendation: processes 4, services 3, certifications 2 — roughly
  proportional to pool size, keeps certifications feasible with margin, and
  yields 15 + 8 + 4 = 27 test-side value-holdout occurrences, enough to build
  the probe.

  ---

  6. Split-support minima analysis

  ┌────────────────────────┬───────────┬──────────┬────────────────┐

  ├────────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────┼────────────────┤

  ├────────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────┼────────────────┤
  │ train≥2, test≥1        │        20 │       11 │              4 │
  ├────────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────┼────────────────┤
  │ train≥3, test≥1        │        16 │        7 │              3 │
  ├────────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────┼────────────────┤
  │ train≥1, test≥2        │        12 │        6 │              1 │
  ├────────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────┼────────────────┤
  │ train≥2, test≥2        │        12 │        6 │              1 │
  ├────────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────┼────────────────┤
  │ train≥3, dev≥1, test≥2 │         6 │        2 │ 0 — infeasible │
  └────────────────────────┴───────────┴──────────┴────────────────┘

  Why test_support = 0 is disqualifying: the value-held-out probe measures
  whether a test-side item can be answered with a value never seen in training.
  A value with no test support contributes zero probe items, so holding it out
  costs training coverage and measures nothing — exactly README:716-718's
  warning. ISO 26262 (7 rows, all train), ISO/SAE 21434, Ford Q1 and ASI Chain 
  of Custody are all in this category; three of the four are certifications.

  Recommendation: train ≥ 2, test ≥ 1. train ≥ 2 ensures the value was genuinely
  learnable before removal and structurally guarantees ≥2 supporting companies.
  test ≥ 2 would be scientifically preferable but reduces the certification
  pool to 1, making k=2 impossible — the probe axis would collapse. No dev
  minimum: dev is not the measurement side here, though dev support is still
  recorded per README:715.

  ---

  7. Proposed deterministic value-selection policy

  PROPOSED ONLY — NOT FROZEN

  support unit          row occurrences (band 3–15, minimum support 3)
  [README:684]
  split minima          train >= 2  AND  test >= 1
  attribute floor       85% train-side rows retain the attribute (union of
  holdouts)
  per-value threshold   same 85%, applied per value
  counts                processes 4 · services 3 · certifications 2
  objective             maximise total test support (probe power)
  tie-breaks            1) fewest train rows lost  2) fewest dev rows lost
                        3) lexicographic value name

  No semantic cherry-picking: the objective and tie-breaks fully determine the
  set.

  Selected — processes (17/148 lost, coverage 88.5%, 15 test items)

  ┌─────────────────────────┬───────┬───────┬─────┬──────┐
  │          value          │ total │ train │ dev │ test │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────┼───────┼─────┼──────┤
  │ Battery Module Assembly │     8 │     3 │   1 │    4 │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────┼───────┼─────┼──────┤
  │ Battery Pack Assembly   │     7 │     2 │   1 │    4 │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────┼───────┼─────┼──────┤
  │ Cell Testing            │    10 │     5 │   1 │    4 │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────┼───────┼─────┼──────┤
  │ Welding                 │    15 │    11 │   1 │    3 │
  └─────────────────────────┴───────┴───────┴─────┴──────┘

  Selected — services (11/148 lost, coverage 92.6%, 8 test items)

  ┌─────────────────────┬───────┬───────┬─────┬──────┐
  │        value        │ total │ train │ dev │ test │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────┼───────┼─────┼──────┤
  │ Battery Collection  │     5 │     2 │   1 │    2 │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────┼───────┼─────┼──────┤
  │ Battery Engineering │    15 │     9 │   2 │    4 │
  ├─────────────────────┼───────┼───────┼─────┼──────┤
  │ Battery Repurposing │     5 │     2 │   1 │    2 │
  └─────────────────────┴───────┴───────┴─────┴──────┘

  Selected — certifications (6/123 lost, coverage 95.1%, 4 test items)

  ┌─────────────┬───────┬───────┬─────┬──────┐
  │    value    │ total │ train │ dev │ test │
  ├─────────────┼───────┼───────┼─────┼──────┤
  │ ISO 22301   │     3 │     2 │   0 │    1 │
  ├─────────────┼───────┼───────┼─────┼──────┤
  │ OHSAS 18001 │     8 │     5 │   0 │    3 │
  └─────────────┴───────┴───────┴─────┴──────┘

  Runners-up

  - processes: {BMA, BPA, Cell Testing, Milling} — equal test power (15) but 18
    rows lost vs 17, loses on tie-break 1. {BMA, BPA, Battery Recycling 
    Logistics, Cell Testing} — 14 test items, only 8 rows lost (94.6%); loses on
    the primary objective but is the best low-collateral option.
  - services: {Battery Collection, Battery Engineering, Circular Economy 
    Consulting} — identical 8/11; loses on lexicographic tie-break only.
  - certifications: {AS9100, OHSAS 18001} — equal test power (4) but 11 rows
    lost vs 6 (91.1%).

  ⚠ Diversity concern I want to flag. Maximising test support concentrates the
  selection in the battery/EV semantic cluster — 3 of 4 processes and all 3
  services are battery-related. This is an artefact of battery values being
  test-heavy, not of cherry-picking, but it means the probe measures
  slot-transfer mostly within one domain. If you prefer semantic spread, the
  low-collateral runner-up set or an added "no more than 2 values sharing a
  leading token" constraint would diversify at some cost to probe power. This is
  a scientific choice I am not making for you.

  ---

  8. Operation-held-out proposal

  README Phase 22 names the constructs — GROUP BY, LIMIT, argmax/top-k — and
  freezes the validation ("appear 0 times across all relevant training gold —
  checked in D's SQL text and in C's metadata"). It does not state whether all
  three are held out together or are a candidate list, and it does not resolve
  the overlap you identified.

  The overlap is real: a top-k query is normally ORDER BY … DESC LIMIT k, so it
  contains LIMIT. Without precedence, one task counts as two held-out families
  and the "0 occurrences" assertion becomes ambiguous.

  Proposed taxonomy — exactly one operation_family per task, assigned by 
  precedence (highest first):

  1. argmax_topk   ORDER BY present AND LIMIT present
  2. group_by      GROUP BY present (and not 1)
  3. limit_only    LIMIT present without ORDER BY
  4. filter_scalar / filter_set / join_membership / count …   (non-held-out
  families)

  Fail-closed companion rule: any task whose SQL matches more than one held-out
  family's defining construct is excluded from training entirely rather than
  assigned to one of them. That keeps exposure_count == 0 unambiguous.

  Open question for you: whether all three families are held out, or a subset.
  Holding out all three removes aggregation, ranking and limiting from training
  simultaneously, which is a strong claim; holding out only argmax_topk is the
  narrowest. Not frozen by me.

  ---

  9. Compositional-held-out proposal

  Pair support (rows/companies having both components non-empty):

  ┌────────────────────┬──────────────┬────────────┬───────────────────────┐
  │                    │    rows      │ companies  │   both components     │
  │        pair        │  T/Tr/Dv/Ts  │  T/Tr/Ts   │     individually      │
  │                    │              │            │    train-visible?     │
  ├────────────────────┼──────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ {processes,        │ 205 / 148 /  │ 193 / 141  │ yes (148, 148)        │
  │ services}          │ 17 / 40      │ / 35       │                       │
  ├────────────────────┼──────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ {processes,        │ 171 / 123 /  │ 160 / 116  │ yes (148, 123)        │
  │ certifications}    │ 14 / 34      │ / 30       │                       │
  ├────────────────────┼──────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ {services,         │ 171 / 123 /  │ 160 / 116  │ yes (148, 123)        │
  │ certifications}    │ 14 / 34      │ / 30       │                       │
  ├────────────────────┼──────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ {processes,        │ 202 / 146 /  │ 190 / 139  │ yes (148, 146)        │
  │ county}            │ 17 / 39      │ / 34       │                       │
  ├────────────────────┼──────────────┼────────────┼───────────────────────┤
  │ {certifications,   │ 169 / 121 /  │ 158 / 114  │ yes (123, 146)        │
  │ county}            │ 14 / 34      │ / 30       │                       │
  └────────────────────┴──────────────┴────────────┴───────────────────────┘

  All candidate pairs have ample support on every split, and every component 
  remains individually train-visible — Phase 23's requirement that "training
  must already contain the required join arity and constructs through other
  combinations" is satisfiable.

  Recommendation

  - Arity 2. README's only worked example is arity-2 ({process, certification})
    contrasted against an arity-3 training item. Higher arity is not excluded by
    the text, but nothing requires it, and arity 2 keeps the superset rule easy
    to audit.
  - Families: the three attribute-pairs {processes, services}, {processes, 
    certifications}, {services, certifications}.
  - Support minima: train ≥ 20 rows and test ≥ 10 rows per pair — all three
    clear this by a wide margin.
  - Number held out: 1 pair. Holding out two of three leaves only one pairwise
    combination in training, which weakens the "training already contains the
    arity through other combinations" premise.
  - Deterministic selection: choose the pair with the lowest train support
    (least collateral), tie-break lexicographic → {services, certifications}
    (train 123, tied with {processes, certifications} at 123; services <
    processes lexicographically... note both tie at 123 rows, so the
    lexicographic tie-break on the sorted pair name decides).

  PROPOSED ONLY — NOT FROZEN. Superset rule enforced as README states: for
  held-out H and every training set T, assert not H ⊆ T, which additionally
  forbids any arity-3 training item containing the held-out pair.

  ---

  10. logical_fingerprint_v1 proposal

  Purpose: identify tasks that are semantically the same question regardless of
  surface form, so few-shot demonstrations and probe items cannot collide
  (README Ph27: "no logical fingerprint shared with any probe item").

  INCLUDED (semantic)          operation_family
                               sorted(fields_used)
                               sorted(values_used)          literal filter
  values
                               sorted(target_columns)
                               answer_type
                               join_arity
                               sorted(required_constructs)
                               entity_dependent

  EXCLUDED (non-semantic)      question wording / paraphrase
                               SQL formatting, whitespace, alias spelling
                               column order in SELECT
                               example_id, dataset path, file name
                               split, split_group, condition, seed
                               row_id unless the question names a record

  canonical form   JSON, sort_keys=True, separators=(",",":"),
  ensure_ascii=False,
                   every list sorted, no floats
  fingerprint      "lfp1:" + sha256(canonical_utf8).hexdigest()[:32]

  Same fingerprint (synthetic):
  - SELECT company FROM companies WHERE county='Hall County' and SELECT 
    c.company FROM companies AS c WHERE c.county = "Hall County" — alias,
    quoting and whitespace differ only.
  - "Which companies are in Hall County?" and "List the companies located in
    Hall County." — paraphrase only.

  Different fingerprint (synthetic):
  - …WHERE county='Hall County' vs …WHERE county='Cobb County' — values_used
    differs.
  - SELECT company … vs SELECT COUNT(*) … over the same filter — answer_type and
    target_columns differ.

  ---

  11. Few-shot eligibility proposal

  A demonstration is eligible only if all hold; anything unprovable ⇒ ineligible
  (fail closed):

  split            every referenced row is train-side (Phase 3 frozen split)
  value            contains no held-out value literal, in question, SQL, answer
  or rationale
  operation        operation_family is not a held-out family, and its SQL
  contains no
                   held-out defining construct
  composition      its component set C satisfies: no held-out set H with H ⊆ C
  fingerprint      logical_fingerprint collides with no probe item
  entity           no held-out entity literal
  schema/catalogue any schema text or value catalogue shipped with the
  demonstration is
                   filtered to training-visible values only
  rendering        the eligibility scan runs on the FINAL rendered chat
  messages, after
                   system prompt, catalogue and template application — not on
  the source object

  ---

  12. answer_type / target_columns / MULTI_PART

  Already frozen by Phase 7 (inspected read-only in grade_v3.py):

  ANSWER_TYPES = ('set', 'scalar', 'top_k', 'multi_part')
  STATUSES     = ('correct','incorrect','generation_failure','parse_failure',
                  'SQL_error','timeout','truncated_output','invalid_output')
  target_columns: non-empty list[str]; scalar requires exactly 1
  set: project then dedupe, order irrelevant · top_k: order and length
  preserved, never deduped

  Still open for Phase 9: the permanent MULTI_PART serialization, which Phase 7
  deliberately deferred (MULTI_PART_ENCODING_NOTE: "This is deliberately NOT a
  permanent generator format. Phase 9/12 owns that decision").

  My assessment, stated plainly: the current Phase 7 stand-in is not suitable as
  the permanent contract. It treats each entry of target_columns as one
  required part, which expresses only column-wise multi-part ("company and
  county"). It cannot express a multi-part question whose parts are different
  operations over possibly different columns — e.g. "how many companies are in
  Hall County, and which of them hold ISO 9001?", where part 1 is a scalar count
  and part 2 is a set. Promoting the stand-in would silently forbid that task
  shape in Phase 12.

  Recommended direction (not frozen): give a multi-part task an explicit parts: 
  [ {part_id, answer_type, target_columns}, … ], with target_columns retained as
  the flattened union for backward compatibility with the Phase 7 grader. This
  needs your decision because it changes the Phase 12 generator contract.

  ---

  13. Exposure-ledger proposal

  An exposure = any occurrence of a held-out literal, construct or component-set
  in a model-visible fine-tuning string, measured on the final rendered form.

  scanned surfaces   CPT passages (A) · system prompts · user messages ·
  assistant targets
                     · SQL text · schema descriptions · value catalogues /
  enumerations
                     · few-shot demonstrations · combined-dataset renderings
  (BC, BD)
                     · repeated / budget-matched renderings (each repeat
  counted)
  scan point         AFTER chat-template rendering, system-prompt insertion,
  catalogue
                     insertion and target rendering — a source object passing is
  insufficient

  ┌─────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  holdout    │                         detection                         │
  │    kind     │                                                           │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │             │ case-sensitive exact literal match on the canonical term, │
  │ value       │  plus a case-insensitive pass reported separately as a    │
  │             │ warning                                                   │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ operation   │ defining construct present in D's SQL text and in C's     │
  │             │ operation_family metadata (README Ph22 requires both)     │
  ├─────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ composition │ rendered task's component set C tested against every      │
  │             │ held-out H: violation iff H ⊆ C                           │
  └─────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  exposure_count = number of rendered training strings containing at least one
  occurrence, reported per held-out item and per arm (A, B, C, D, BC, BD).

  Required invariant: exposure_count == 0 for every frozen holdout across every
  arm. Non-zero ⇒ phase fails, no artifact written.

  ---

  ┌──────────────────┬──────────────────────┬───────────┬──────────────────┐
  │   perturbation   │      processes       │ services  │  certifications  │

  │                  │ CHANGED (Welding →   │           │                  │

  │                  │ Logistics)           │           │                  │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────────────┤

  │ higher)          │                      │           │  1 < k 2)        │
  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────────────┤

  ├──────────────────┼──────────────────────┼───────────┼──────────────────┤
  │ k + 1            │ baseline + 1         │ baseline  │ baseline + 1     │
  │                  │                      │ + 1       │                  │
  └──────────────────┴──────────────────────┴───────────┴──────────────────┘

  The proposal is not brittle: it is invariant to the support-unit question
  entirely, and to a −5pp floor change. The two genuine sensitivities are the
  90% floor (swaps one process value) and test ≥ 2 (collapses the certification
  axis) — both are exactly the parameters flagged for your decision.
